Car Repair Billing Procedures Manual    176

                                                                            (Last Updated: 1/1/2025)

Appendix V                    Resubmitting a Revised Invoice Which was
              Returned Unpaid Per AAR Office Manual Rule 112
How to resubmit an AAR Revised Bill via the AAR/Railinc Data Exchange system
Listed below are guidelines for resubmitting a detailed revised AAR bill that was improperly rendered
per Rule 112 or had errors or questionable charges exceeding amounts shown in Rule 112.
V.1           Detail AAR Billing Repair Data Records
Listed below are guidelines for resubmitting the original detailed revised invoice from a returned unpaid
submitted invoice. An AAR bill improperly rendered per Rule 112 or had errors or questionable charges
exceeding amounts shown in Rule 112 and returned unpaid back from the “Billed Party”. When
submitting (Detail Source “BR”) AAR billing repair data records to Data Exchange, the following fields
must be filled in as required based on the repairs performed. Original invoice repairs must be submitted
as a standalone invoice and must not be included with a subsequent invoice in later months.
•     Record Format (must be "1")
•     Billing Party/Invoicing Party           •     Applied Qualifier
•     Billed Party                            •     Why Made Code
•     Account Date (YYMM)                     •     Removed Job Code
•     Invoice Number                          •     Removed Qualifier
•     Price Master Currency Indicator         •     Responsibility Code
•     Detail Source                           •     Labor Charge
•     Document Reference Number               •     Material Charge
•     Car Initial                             •     Material Sign
•     Car Number                              •     Machine Priceable Indicator set to “Y” if the
•     Kind of Car Symbol                            AAR price master was used to edit/price the
•     Load/Empty Indicator                          record, otherwise set to “N”
•     Repair Date (YYMMDD)                    •     Wrong Repair Indicator
•     SPLC                                    •     Narrative & Wheel Information set to some
•     Repairing Party                               meaningful description as defined in C.3
•     Repairing Party Invoice Number          •     Labor Rate
•     Repairing Document Reference Number     •     Expanded SPLC
•     Repair Facility Type                    •     Line Number
•     Location on Car                         •     Resubmitted Invoice Indicator = “R”
•     Quantity                                •     Original Invoice Number
•     Condition Code                          •     AAR Component ID (CID)
•     Applied Job Code                        •     Original Account Date








Car Repair Billing Procedures Manual    177

The following fields must be filled in as indicated below:
Billing Party/Invoicing Party must be filled in with the billing party’s initial
Billed Party must be filled in with the billed party’s initial
Account Date (YYMM) must be filled in with the billing party’s account date (year/month format)
Invoice Number must be filled in with the billing party's invoice number
Detail Source must always be set to "BR" for AAR billing repair data billing
Repairing Party must be filled in with the repairing party’s initial
Repairing Party Invoice Number must be filled in with the repairing party’s invoice number
Repairing Party Document Reference Number, if present, must be filled in with the repairing party’s
document reference number
Resubmitted Invoice Indicator = “R”
Below is an example of an invoice with Rule 112 errors and requirements for submitting revised records.
V.1.1  Rule 112 Example 1—Improperly Rendered Invoice
Railroad XXXX repairs railcar owner ZZZZ during Railroad XXXX’s account date 1001 (January 2010) and
inadvertently invoices road YYYY under Invoice Number “123456”. Railcar owner YYYY takes exception
to this invoice as improperly rendered and notifies Railroad XXXX. Railroad XXXX resubmits revised or
cancels invoice and submits a new invoice (987654) to ZZZZ. invoice to ZZZZ. Because the data has
already passed through Railinc Data Exchange one time Railroad XXXX indicates “R” in Resubmitted
Invoice Indicator field.
Railroad XXXX submits the incorrect original repair record to the Data Exchange system with the
following fields filled in:
Billing Party/Invoicing Party = “XXXX”
Billed Party = “YYYY” (Note: this was incorrect and should have been ZZZZ)
Account Date (YYMM) = "1401" or “1402”
Invoice Number = "123456" (Note: It is not required to use the original invoice number.) or “987654”
Detail Source = "BR" (Billing repair data)
Document Reference Number = spaces (not filled in)
Repairing Party = “XXXX”
Repairing Party Invoice Number = "123456"
Repairing Party Document Reference Number = "123456" or “987654” if a new invoice was submitted
& old one canceled.
The rest of the fields on the Format 1 record are filled in based on the repairs performed.
Railcar owner YYYY notifies Railroad XXXX that the invoices are being returned unpaid because of
improper Billed Party. All BRCs were for cars owned by Railcar owner ZZZZ.







Car Repair Billing Procedures Manual    178

Railroad XXXX resubmits revised or cancels invoice and submits a new invoice “987654” to proper car
owner ZZZZ to the Data Exchange system with the following fields to the Data Exchange system during
Account Date 1402 with the following fields filled in and indicates “R” in Resubmitted Invoice Indicator
field for all records in the invoice, Account Month can have the original Account Month or the Account
Month in which the data is submitted, “1402”.
Billing Party/Invoicing Party = “XXXX”
Billed Party = “ZZZZ” (Note: this is the revised Billed Party)
Account Date (YYMM) = "1402"
Invoice Number = "123456" (Note: It is not required to use the original invoice number.)
Detail Source = "BR"
Document Reference Number = spaces (not filled in)
Repairing Party = “XXXX”
Repairing Party Invoice Number = "123456" or “987654” if a new invoice was submitted & old one
canceled.
Repairing Party Document Reference Number = spaces (not filled in)
Line Number = “00001”
Resubmitted Invoice Indicator = “R” (Note: Because the data has already passed through Railinc Data
Exchange one time Railroad XXXX indicates “R” in Resubmitted Invoice Indicator field)
Original Invoice Number = “123456” (Note: Original Invoice Number that was first submitted through
Data Exchange)
Original Account Date (YYMM) = “1001” (Note: Original Account Date that was first submitted through
Data Exchange)
The rest of the fields on the Format 1 record are filled in based on the repairs performed.
V.1.2    Rule 112 Example 2—Questionable Charges Invoice
Railroad XXXX repairs railcar owner YYYY during Railroad XXXX’s account date 1401 under Invoice
Number “123456”. Invoice total is $5000.00. Railcar owner YYYY reviews charges and notes exceptions
on the BRC for car YYYY 012345 in the net amount of $1000.00 (Exception taken for labor charge of 13.0
hours for Job Code 4450, Qualifier “MT”). This exception amount exceeds the threshold noted in AAR
Office Manual Rule 112. Railcar owner YYYY notifies Railroad XXXX of the questionable charges and that
the invoice is being returned unpaid. If Railroad XXXX agrees with exceptions from Car Owner YYYY,
XXXX resubmits revised invoice revised or cancels invoice and submits a new invoice
(987654) less lines of the exceptions removed from data submission), to the Data Exchange system
during Account Date 1402 with the following fields filled in:
Billing Party/Invoicing Party = “XXXX”
Billed Party = “YYYY”
Account Date (YYMM) = "1401"
Invoice Number = "123456"
Detail Source = "BR" (Billing repair data)
Document Reference Number = spaces (not filled in)
Repairing Party = “XXXX”
Repairing Party Invoice Number = "123456"
Repairing Party Document Reference Number = spaces (not filled in)


Car Repair Billing Procedures Manual    179

The rest of the fields on the Format 1 record are filled in based on the repairs performed.
Railcar owner YYYY notifies Railroad XXXX that the invoices are being returned unpaid because of
questionable charges exceeding the threshold of AAR Office Manual Rule 112.
Railroad XXXX resubmits revised invoice or cancels invoice and submits a new invoice (987654) during
account date 1402 to the Data Exchange system with the following fields filled in and indicates “R” in
the Resubmitted Invoice Indicator field for all records in the invoice. Account Month can have the
Original Account Month or the Account Month in which the data is submitted, “1402”.
Billing Party/Invoicing Party = “XXXX”
Billed Party = “YYYY”
Account Date (YYMM) = “1401” or "1402"
Invoice Number = "123456" (Note: It is not required to use the original invoice number.) or “987654”
Detail Source = "BR" (Billing repair data)
Document Reference Number = spaces (not filled in)
Car Initial = “YYYY”
Car Number = “012345”
Repairing Party = XXXX
Repairing Party Invoice Number = "123456" or “987654” if a new invoice was submitted & old one
canceled.
Repairing Party Document Reference Number = spaces (not filled in)
Line Number = “00001”
Resubmitted Invoice Indicator = “R” (Note: Because the data has already passed through Railinc Data
Exchange one time, Railroad XXXX indicates “R” in Resubmitted Invoice Indicator field)
Original Invoice Number = “123456” (Note: Original Invoice Number that was first submitted through
Data Exchange)
Original Account Date (YYMM) = “1401” (Note: Original Account Date that was first submitted through
Data Exchange)
The rest of the fields on the Format 1 record are filled in based on the repairs performed.
V.1.3     Rule 112 Example 3—Rejected Invoice, Resubmitted with Additional
          Billing Repair Data Added to a New Invoice in a Future Billing Month
Railroad XXXX repairs railcar owner YYYY during Railroad XXXX’s account date 1401(January 2010) under
Invoice Number “123456”. Invoice total is $5000.00. Railcar owner YYYY reviews charges and notes
exceptions on the BRC for car YYYY 012345 in the net amount of $1000.00 (Exception was an excessive
labor charge of 13.0 hours for Job Code 4450, Qualifier “MT”). The revised fields are completed as
follows:
Railroad XXXX submits the incorrect original repair record to the Data Exchange system with the
following fields filled in:
Billing Party/Invoicing Party = “XXXX”
Billed Party = “YYYY”
Account Date (YYMM) = "1401"
Invoice Number = "123456"

Car Repair Billing Procedures Manual                                                                180

Detail Source = "BR" (Billing repair data)
Document Reference Number = spaces (not filled in)
Repairing Party = “XXXX”
Repairing Party Invoice Number = "123456"
Repairing Party Document Reference Number = spaces (not filled in)
The rest of the fields on the Format 1 record are filled in based on the repairs performed.
Railcar owner YYYY notifies Railroad XXXX that the invoice is being returned unpaid because of
questionable charges exceeding the threshold of AAR Office Manual Rule 112. Railroad XXXX submits to
Data Exchange the revised BRC(s) on a future Invoice account date 1403 under Invoice Number
“234567” with other BRC(s) that were not previously submitted.
If Railroad XXXX agrees with exceptions from Car Owner YYYY, XXXX resubmits revised invoice (less lines
of exception), to the Data Exchange system with the following fields and indicates “R” in Resubmitted
Invoice Indicator field for all records in the invoice that were previously submitted.
Billing Party/Invoicing Party = “XXXX”
Billed Party = “YYYY”
Account Date (YYMM) = "1003"
Invoice Number = "234567" (Note: It is not required to use the original invoice number.)
Detail Source = "BR" (Billing repair data)
Document Reference Number = spaces (not filled in)
Car Initial = “YYYY”
Car Number = “012345”
Repairing Party = XXXX
Repairing Party Invoice Number = "234567"
Repairing Party Document Reference Number = spaces (not filled in)
Line Number = “00001”
Resubmitted Invoice Indicator = “R” (Note: Because the data has already passed through Railinc Data
Exchange one time, Railroad XXXX indicates “R” in Resubmitted Invoice Indicator field)
Original Invoice Number = “123456” (Note: Original Invoice Number that was first submitted through
Data Exchange)
Original Account Date (YYMM) = “1001” (Note: Original Account Date that was first submitted through
Data Exchange)
The rest of the fields on the Format 1 record are filled in based on the repairs performed.

Appendix W                    Joint Inspection/Defect Card Billing for
              Retired/Dismantled Unit (Rule 102.D.9)
How to submit joint inspection certificate (JIC) or Defect Card billing via the AAR/Railinc Data
Exchange system when a car owner elects to retire/dismantle a damaged car instead of making
repairs (per Office Manual Rule 102.D.9)
When an owner receives a car home with a defect card for unfair usage damage or is in possession of a
car that has an endorsed JIC, and the owner elects to retire/dismantle instead of repair car and/or rack,
charges representing estimated repairs may be made for material and labor as would have been
required for repairing or renewing items actually listed on defect card/JIC. For JIC billing in this case,
detail source code “JD” (Joint Inspection/Defect Card Billing for Retired/Dismantled Unit) must be used
when a car owner elects to retire/dismantle a damaged car instead of making repairs (per Office Manual
Rule 102.D.9).
Important Note:          Detail Source “JD” (Joint Inspection/Defect Card Billing for Retired/Dismantled Unit),
                         is only to be used when a car owner elects to retire/dismantle a damaged car
                         instead of making repairs (per Office Manual Rule 102.D.9).
When submitting detail JIC original records to Data Exchange the following fields must be filled in as
required based on the repairs performed.
•     Record Format (must be "1")         •     Applied Job Code
•     Billing/Invoicing Party             •     Applied Qualifier
•     Billed Party                        •     Why Made Code
•     Account Date (YYMM)                 •     Removed Job Code
•     Invoice Number                      •     Removed Qualifier
•     Price Master Currency Indicator     •     Responsibility Code
•     Detail Source                       •     Defect Card/JIC Party
•     Car Initial                         •     Defect Card/JIC Date
•     Car Number                          •     Labor Charge
•     Kind of Car Symbol                  •     Material Charge
•     Load/Empty Indicator                •     Material Sign
•     Repair Date (YYMMDD)                •     Machine Priceable Indicator
•     SPLC                                •     Wrong Repair Indicator
•     Repair Facility Type                •     Narrative & Wheel Information set to some
•     Repairing Party                           meaningful description as defined in C.3
•     Repairing Party Invoice Number      •     Labor Rate
•     Location on Car                     •     Expanded SPLC
•     Quantity                            •     Line Number
•     Condition Code






Car Repair Billing Procedures Manual    191

The following fields must be filled in as indicated below:
Billing/Invoicing Party must be filled in with the repairing party’s initial
Billed Party must be filled in with the billed party’s initial
Account Date must be filled in with the repairing party’s account date (year/month format)
Invoice Number must be filled in with the repairing party's invoice number
Detail Source must always be set to "JD" for JIC Joint Inspection/Defect Card Billing (Retired/Dismantled
 Unit)
Document Reference Number is conditional. Only required for Detail Source “CB”. Required for CBA
 number. It could be used to hold a file number or some information that would help the Billing or
 Billed Party.
Repairing Party must be set to the repairing party’s initial
Repairing Party Invoice Number must be set to the repairing party’s invoice number
Repairing Party Document Reference Number, if present, must be set to the repairing party’s document
 reference number
Example 1:  Railroad YYYY damages car XXXX123456 owned by Car Owner XXXX on November 3, 2013.
            While in possession of XXXX123456, Car Owner XXXX performs a financial analysis and elects
            to retire/dismantle XXXX123456 under authority of endorsed JIC dated November 15, 2013
            from Railroad YYYY. Car Owner XXXX submits the billing record representing estimated
            repair costs during Car Owner’s January 2014 account month to the Data Exchange system
            and fills in the following fields:
Billing/Invoicing Party = "XXXX"
Billed Party = "YYYY"
Account Date = "1401"
Invoice Number = "12345"
Detail Source = "JD" (Joint Inspection/Defect Card Billing - Retired/Dismantled Unit)
Document Reference Number = spaces (not filled in)
Repairing Party = "XXXX"
Repairing Party Invoice Number = "12345"
Repairing Party Document Reference Number = spaces (not filled in)
Defect Card/JIC Party = “YYYY”
Defect Card/JIC Date = “131115”
The rest of the fields on the Format 1 record are filled in based on the repairs as would have been
required.

Appendix X                  AAR Component ID (CID) Reporting
How to submit AAR Component ID billing repair data via the AAR/Railinc Data Exchange
system to be billed during the Monthly Billing Cycle.
Note: Refer to Appendix W for reporting components under Detail Source JD.

                           Component Tracking Reporting Requirement Dates Table
               Component                         DX Informational Error         DX Mandatory Reject
  Wheel Sets (FM 44)                       January 1st 2013                    October 1st 2013
  Couplers, Side frames, Bolsters (FM      January 1st 2014                    July 1st 2014
  16, 17, 18, 47, 48)
  Service portion, Emergency portion       January 1st 2016                    July 1st 2016
  (FM 4)
  Slack Adjusters (FM 8)                   October 1st 2016                     April 1st 2017
  EOC Cushion Units (FM 59)                November 1st 2020                    April 1st 2021

Important Note: Job Codes requiring a Component ID are identified in the AAR Price Matrix by
the field “CID (Y/N):” where the indicator is shown as “Y” as noted in the example below.
JOB 3333      NEW WHEEL SET 33 INCH, 6 X 11 AXLE                                QLFR - 00      RULE           44

Section F, for Billing Repair data requirements.

Car Repair Billing Procedures Manual                 196

Detail AAR Billing Repair Data Records
When submitting (Detail Source “BR, DC, DR, JI, JR, OT, RE, SH”) AAR billing repair data records to
Data Exchange the following fields must be filled in as required based on the repairs performed:
 •     Record Format (must be "1")         •     Why Made Code
 •     Billing/Invoicing Party             •     Removed Job Code
 •     Billed Party                        •     Removed Qualifier
 •     Account Date (YYMM)                 •     Responsibility Code
 •     Invoice Number                      •     Labor Charge
 •     Price Master Currency Indicator     •     Material Charge
 •     Detail Source                       •     Material Sign
 •     Car Initial                         •     Machine Priceable Indicator set to
 •     Car Number                                “Y” if the AAR price master was used
 •     Kind of Car Symbol                        to edit/price record, otherwise set to
 •     Load/Empty Indicator                      “N”
 •     Repair Date (YYMMDD)                •     Wrong Repair Indicator
 •     SPLC                                •     Narrative & Wheel Information set to
 •     Repairing Party                           some meaningful description as
 •     Repairing Party Invoice Number            defined in C.3
 •     Repair Facility Type                •     Labor Rate
 •     Location on Car                     •     Expanded SPLC
 •     Quantity                            •     Line Number
 •     Condition Code                      •     Resubmitted Invoice Number
 •     Applied Job Code                    •     Original Invoice
 •     Applied Qualifier                   •     Original Account Date
                                           •     AAR Component ID (CID)


The following fields must be filled in as indicated below:
Billing/Invoicing Party must be filled in with the repairing party’s initial
Billed Party must be filled in with the billed party’s initial
Account Date must be filled in with the repairing party’s account date (year/month format)
Invoice Number must be filled in with the repairing party's invoice number
Detail Source must be set to one of the following " BR, DC, DR, JI, JR, OT, RE, SH " for AAR
 billing repair data
Document Reference Number is conditional. It is only required for Detail Source “CB” and is
 required for CBA number. It could be used to hold a file number or some information
 that would help the Billing or Billed Party.
Repairing Party must be set to the repairing party’s initial
Repairing Party Invoice Number must be set to the repairing party’s invoice number
Repairing Party Document Reference Number, if present, must be set to the repairing
 party’s document reference number.
Applied Job Code must be filled with a net applied valid job code.
AAR Component ID (CID), must be filled with the unique alpha numeric component
 identifier for the component being applied. 14 position field.

Example 1: (Note: Not all applicable job codes will be listed in the examples. Not all additional
 job code components required when reporting a wheel will be listed in the
 examples. Refer to FM Rule 44.E.2 for details for all required fields.)
 Railroad XXXX repairs car owner YYYY's railcar during railroad XXXX's March 2014
 account month. Railroad XXXX applies a wheel set with a valid Applied Job Code of
 3336 with AAR Component ID of “ABCD1234567890”. Railroad XXXX submits the

Repairing Party Document Reference Number = spaces (not filled in)
Location on Car = “01”
Applied Job Code = “3336”
AAR Component ID (CID) = “ABCD1234567890”
The rest of the fields on the Format 1 record are filled in based on the repairs performed.

Car Repair Billing Procedures Manual    204

                                                                                 (Last Updated: 7/1/2026)
Appendix Y  Exceptions and Counter Billing Authority per
         AAR Office Manual Rule 112.D, 112.H and 120.B
IMPORTANT: Effective January 1, 2026, use of the CRB Electronic Exceptions System is mandatory for
submitting billing exceptions, issuing responses, and CBAs (Counter Billing Authority).
When a billed party receives an invoice where the Record Type 1 tax record is submitted in accordance
with Appendix N, including car initial and number, tax-related exceptions must be submitted at the
individual car level using the CRB Electronic Exceptions System as defined below.
When a billed party receives an invoice where the railcar initial and number are missing from Tax Billing
Record Type 1 (Detail Sources PT, QT, GT, HT and IV) and/or aggregate tax billing is submitted as a
Record Type 8 (Detail Source ZZ), the billed party may submit an exception via email to the billing party
referencing AAR Circular Letter C-14679. After which, a Circular Letter CBA may be issued by the billing
party.
Exceptions are a disagreement with a BRC (Billing Repair Card, as defined in Rule 83 exhibit “A”) line
item, or to an entire BRC, that is brought to the attention of the Billing Party by the Billed Party. Billing
exceptions may include, but are not limited to, such issues as incorrect car initials/numbers, incorrect
repair dates, incorrect pricing, AAR rule violations, invalid repairs based on car type or car configuration,
repairs related to EHMS alerts where no alert is found in the EHMS data, excessive quantities, excessive
hours, or excessive material charges.
Y.1      How to Submit Exceptions to a Billing Party for Charges
         Received for AAR Car Repair Billing
This section lists guidelines for submitting exceptions to a Billing Party.
There are several types of exceptions to AAR Car Repair Bills:
 1.     Unpaid Invoices – follow AAR OM Rule 112.H
 2.     Paid Invoices – follow AAR OM Rule 112.H.4
Exceptions and exception communications are handled using the CRB Electronic Exceptions System and
are processed in real time. Electronic billing exceptions will be submitted through the CRB Electronic
Exceptions System, and once validated, electronically forwarded to the appropriate billing party.
Electronic exceptions are herein referred to as “Exception Letters”. Billing parties will use this same
system to respond to exception letters and either agree, partially agree, or disagree with each
exception. Billing parties will also use this system to issue CBAs (Counter Billing Authority) to a billed
party when in agreement or partial agreement on a paid invoice as defined in Y.2.
Exception letters shall be submitted at the invoice level and submitted either through an FTP (File
Transfer Protocol) mailbox or through an upload function using the CRB Electronic Exceptions Upload on
the CRB Dashboard. In either case, every inbound file will be validated before accepted in accordance
with Y.1.2. Validated messages will be stored and forwarded to the appropriate receiver. Invalid
messages will not be saved and will be rejected. In both cases, the CRB participant(s) listed to receive
exception submission notifications for the submitting party will be notified via email of the message
validation status. Participant notifications are managed in CRB under the Data Exchange tab and are the
responsibility of the participant CRB Content Admin to maintain.

Car Repair Billing Procedures Manual                                                                       205

All exception letters submitted shall be formatted in accordance with Y.1.4. Exception letters submitted
through an FTP mailbox shall be in in a 500-byte format. Both .txt and .dat files are accepted. Electronic
exceptions submitted through the CRB exceptions upload function on the dashboard shall be in .xlsx
format. In both cases, the exception message shall be in a .zip file with all applicable attachments. All
exception letter submissions and responses shall use the file naming convention as per Y.1.3.
Each Record Format 2 message allows for 1 attachment per record. There is no limit to the number of
attachments in the .zip file, however no single attachment can exceed 10 MB. The attachment field is
optional and can be used for supporting documentation. Only PDF documents are accepted in the
electronic exceptions system. Applicable attachments should be included in the .zip file and the file
name of the attachment should be listed in the Record Format 2 message. The exception file will be
rejected if any attachments in the .zip file are not correctly listed in the exception message, or an
attachment is listed in the message but is not included in the .zip file. File names in the exception
message and .zip file must match for validation.
Unpaid Invoices (Invoice Rejection)
Per AAR Office Manual Rule 112.H.3.a, “The unpaid invoice must be returned to the Billing Party no later
than 60 days from availability of the data (as defined in Rule 112.H.2).” Exception letters submitted for
an unpaid invoice (Invoice Rejection) may be submitted as a header level invoice rejection (no identified
exceptions). This is where the entire unpaid invoice is rejected at the header level and no exceptions are
identified. When a header level invoice rejection is submitted, the billed party must document in the
description section of the message why the invoice is being rejected. The billing party is limited to only
one response on header level invoice rejections to indicate agreement or disagreement.
In lieu of a header level invoice rejection, exception letters may be submitted for an unpaid invoice
(Invoice Rejection) as a non-header level invoice rejection. A non-header level unpaid invoice rejection
must contain all exceptions leading to the invoice rejection for billing party review. Identifying
exceptions at the invoice rejection level does not prevent a billed party from identifying additional
exceptions or taking exceptions once an invoice is paid. In addition, identifying exceptions on an invoice
rejection does not circumvent AAR Office Manual Rule 112.H.4.a.
Paid Invoices
Exception letters submitted for a paid invoice, in accordance with AAR Office Manual Rule 112.H, can be
at the BRC Header level and Repair Line level. This exception letter is submitted for billing party
consideration of reducing the dollar amount charged. If the exception letter response from the Billing
Party (to whom the exception was taken) is not agreed to, a second letter of exception may be
submitted debating the issue of charges on outstanding exceptions. Subsequent letters of exception for
the same invoice may not contain any new exceptions that were not identified in the first exception
letter submitted for the paid invoice. The exception letter count is limited to a maximum of 99 exception
letters and 99 responses.
Here is an example of multiple exception letters and responses on the same invoice as defined in Y.1.3:
Note: Initial exception letter must always be 01. Each exception letter after on the same invoice must be
previous exception letter +1.
 •     ABCD_WXYZ_123456_2409_01 (Initial exception letter from billed party to billing party)

 •     WXYZ_ABCD_123456_2409_01 (Response from billing party to billed party)

Car Repair Billing Procedures Manual                                                                  206

•     ABCD_WXYZ_123456_2409_02 (Second exception letter from billed party to billing party)

•     WXYZ_ABCD_123456_2409_02 (Response to second letter from billing party to billed party)

•     ABCD_WXYZ_123456_2409_03 (Third exception letter from billed party to billing party)

•     WXYZ_ABCD_123456_2409_03 (Response to third letter from billing party to billed party)










Car Repair Billing Procedures Manual    207

Y.1.1  Examples of Exceptions that are Not Valid
This section contains examples of submitted exceptions that are not acceptable:
 •     Exceptions being taken to Wheelsets, Draft Gears, and Brake Beams to verify new material.
       Taking exception using the full price charged. When asked to verify, the amount should be $0 or
       the difference between new and reconditioned.
 •     Air hose not out of date (when it is either A/B end issue, or date last air hose was installed
       combined with age of hose at time of installation meets replacement standards). No verification
       sent with exception.
 •     Repair is out of date (when previously rejected multiple times).
 •     Brake shoe not standard to car (when it is acceptable per AAR rules – also Carmen replace shoes
       in kind).
 •     Material price greater than AAR Price Master (when it is not).
 •     Car does not exist in Umler (when it does or alternately was in Umler at the time of repair).
 •     Not all exceptions filed at the same time.
 •     ABT not out of date-documentation provided (data provided is not supported by Railinc).
 •     Wheel not High Impact at the time of repair-provides graph but no documentation from Railinc.
 •     “Removed Wheel data does not match previous application” – Not rule based, and no
       verification provided of what car owner believed was on the car. Billing Party only knows what
       they applied not what others have billed car owner.
 •     Car Kind Symbol when it is not applicable to repair made.
 •     Air brake hose support complete repair not required to maintain height for a Section A defect
       repair made in accordance with Rule 5.B. Correct Repair Chart to obtain proper height
       requirement.
 •     Requests for supplemental supporting documentation not required in Rule 36 Section E for
       removal of overheated roller bearing (WM 50) and roller bearing temperature performance
       (WM51) or (WM52).
 •     The handling line can setout car, at its discretion, to perform and bill a car for a Rule 91 Service
       Interruption line of road repair to safely accomplish repair instead of slowing train speed and
       moving car to terminal without setout.
 •     Load/Empty when it is not applicable to repair made.
 •     Exceptions to Air Brake tests cannot be taken based on the Qualifier reported.






Car Repair Billing Procedures Manual    208

Y.1.2       Exception Correspondence
This section contains rules for required fields and validations based on exception letter type. Validations
are conducted on fields where a mandatory value is required or when data is present in an optional
field. Validations are divided into Message Format (EX2), Invoice (EXI), and Attachment (EXA)
validations.

Car Repair Billing Procedures Manual    213

Message Validations (EX2) – Validations of the message data

<table>
  <thead>
    <tr>
      <th>Message Field</th>
      <th>Error Code</th>
      <th>Description</th>
    </tr>
  </thead>
  <tbody>
    <tr>
      <td>Record Format</td>
      <td>EX2001</td>
      <td>Invalid Record Format. Exception messages must be a Format 2.</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
      <td>Billing Party</td>
      <td>EX2002</td>
      <td>Billing/Invoicing Party Initial - Must be present and alphanumeric</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
      <td>Billed Party</td>
      <td>EX2003</td>
      <td>Billing/Invoicing Party Initial - Must be present and alphanumeric.</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
      <td>Account Date</td>
      <td>EX2004</td>
      <td>Must be present and valid numeric date format (YYMM).</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
      <td>Total Invoice Amount</td>
      <td>EX2005</td>
      <td>Must be present and numeric. Cannot exceed 16 characters.</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
      <td>Invoice Number</td>
      <td>EX2006</td>
      <td>Must be present and alphanumeric.</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
      <td>Car Initial</td>
      <td>EX2007</td>
      <td>Car initial Must be present and alphabetic.</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
      <td>Car Initial</td>
      <td>EX2008</td>
      <td>Car initial must not exceed 4 characters .</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
      <td>Car Initial</td>
      <td>EX2009</td>
      <td>Car initial must be blank for this type of exception.</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
      <td>Car Number</td>
      <td>EX2010</td>
      <td>Car number must be numeric and greater than zero.</td>
    </tr>

Car Repair Billing Procedures Manual    215

Y.1.3  Exception File Naming Convention
Each exception letter and response shall use the same file naming conventions. The file names are used
to assist in identifying when an exception is submitted, simple identification in the dashboard, and to
associate responses with submissions. Exception letters and the .zip container will both use the same
naming convention as follows:
SubmitterMark_ReceiverMark_InvoiceNumber_AccountDate_ExceptionLetterNumber
<u>Example of a correctly named zip file: </u>
ABCD_WXYZ_123456_2409_01.zip
<u>Example of exception file inside of zip file including supporting attachments: </u>
Note: Spaces in attachment names must be represented by an underscore.
ABCD_WXYZ_123456_2409_01 (.dat or .txt for FTP, .xlsx for CRB upload)
Attachment_1.pdf
Attachment_2.pdf
Attachment_3.pdf
The first submission of an exception to billing, either an unpaid invoice rejection (Detail Source RJ) or a
paid invoice exception (Detail Source EE) must use exception letter number 01 in the naming convention
and the exception file name. Each corresponding submission, on the same invoice, using the same detail
source, must use the next numerical number from the previous submission.
When an invoice rejection is resolved, and a subsequently issued invoice is then paid, the billed party
may still take billing exceptions against the invoice in accordance with AAR Office Manual Rule 112.H.
When this occurs, the submission against the paid invoice (Detail Source EE) must again start with
exception letter 01. Each corresponding submission, on the same invoice, using the same detail source,
must use the next numerical number from the previous submission.
Here is an example of an invoice being rejected and then paid with exceptions:
Note: When the detail source of the exception on the same invoice changes from RJ to EE, the file
names will be identical.
Initial invoice submitted to billed party:
 •     ABCD_WXYZ_123456_2409_01 (Initial invoice rejection from billed party / Detail Source RJ)
 •     WXYZ_ABCD_123456_2409_01 (Response from billing party to billed party)
Invoice reissued to billed party:
 •     ABCD_WXYZ_123456_2409_02 (Second invoice rejection on reissued invoice from billed party /
       Detail Source RJ)
 •     WXYZ_ABCD_123456_2409_02 (Second response from billing party to billed party)

Car Repair Billing Procedures Manual                                                               216

Invoice reissued by billing party and paid by billed party:
 •          ABCD_WXYZ_123456_2409_01 (Initial exception letter from billed party to billing party)
 •          WXYZ_ABCD_123456_2409_01 (Response from billing party to billed party)
 •          ABCD_WXYZ_123456_2409_02 (Second exception letter from billed party to billing party)
 •          WXYZ_ABCD_123456_2409_02 (Response to second letter from billing party to billed party)
Excerpt from the AAR Office Manual
The following is an excerpt from Rule 112.H.4 in the AAR Office Manual. Refer to the actual AAR Office
Manual for the most complete and current information.
112.H. Invoice Processing and Auditing Regulations
      e. All exceptions must be taken at the same time. (Does not apply to exceptions covered
             by time limit guarantee, duplicate billing, wrong repairs per Rule 111, Rule 85 material
             billed per Rule 111, or billing in excess of the car's net settlement value.)
      f.     Billing Repair Data where exceptions are being taken, must match the original data
             and must be presented in the format as defined in Rule 83, Exhibit A. The correct
             information, as shown on the original record of repair, must be furnished.
      g.     When exceptions are taken to a paid invoice, only the documentation for the repair
             event(s) with exceptions should be submitted to the Billing Party.
      h. Exceptions may be taken by one of the following methods. In either case, reference
             must be made to invoice number, date of invoice, and accounting month in which
             charges were included.
             (1) Each exception must be fully detailed in the format defined by Rule 112.H.4.b
                  and must be supported by the document defined in Rule 112.H.4.f.
             (2)  Each exception must be clearly stated and associated to alleged improper line
                  item on face of the document defined in Rule 112.H.4.f.
             (3)  Exceptions must reference the original line number as submitted by the Billing
                  Party.

Car Repair Billing Procedures Manual    219

Y.2     Issuing Electronic Counter Billing Authority (CBA)
This section lists guidelines for issuing electronic CBAs to a receiving party.
There are several reasons a CBA is issued:
 1.     Paid Invoice Exception Response – AAR Rule 112.D
 2.     MID Inspection – AAR Rule 120.B
 3.     AAR Circular Letter – AAR Rule 112.D
Per Rule 112.D, “Counter Billing Authority (CBA) will be used for the purpose of monetary adjustment
for the parties concerned.” CBAs will be issued using the CRB Electronic Exceptions System and
processed in real time. CBAs may be issued in response to paid invoice exception letters (Rule 112.D), as
the result of an MID Inspection (Rule 120.B), or in response to an AAR Circular Letter claim from a
collecting party (Rule 112.D).
All CBA messages shall be formatted in accordance with Y.2.5 as Format 3 messages. Both FTP and the
CRB Exceptions Dashboard can be used to submit CBA messages. CBA messages in response to a paid
exception will be included with the Type 2 messages in the response file while MID and Circular Letter
CBA messages will be submitted as independent messages. All CBA messages submitted through an FTP
mailbox shall be in a 500-byte format. Both .txt and .dat files are accepted. CBA Messages submitted
through the CRB exceptions upload function on the CRB Exceptions Dashboard shall be in .xlsx format. In
all cases, the exception message shall be in a .zip file with all applicable attachments.
When a CBA message is issued in response to a paid invoice exception via FTP in a 500-byte format, the
Format 3 CBA message should be the last record in the response, preceded by the Format 2 records.
When submitted as a .xlsx upload through the exceptions dashboard, the Format 3 CBA Message will be
submitted using the CBA CA Template tab on the “Exception Template with CBA”, available for
download through the CRB Electronic Exceptions Dashboard.
Each Record Format 3 message allows for 3 attachments per record, 1 in each attachment field. There is
no limit to the number of attachments in the .zip file however no single attachment can exceed 10 MB.
The Attachment 1 field is mandatory whereas Attachment 2 and Attachment 3 are optional. Attachment
1 must list the file name of the CBA Letter issued and a copy of the CBA Letter must be included in the
.zip file. Attachment 2 and 3 can be used for any additional attachments the submitter deems needed
for the record. Only PDF documents are accepted in the electronic exceptions system. The CBA file will
be rejected if any attachments in the .zip file are not correctly listed in the CBA message, or an
attachment is listed in the CBA message but is not included in the .zip file. File names in the exception
message and .zip file must match for validation.
Y.2.1   Issuing a Paid Invoice CBA
Exception letters submitted by a billed party to the billing party for a paid invoice, in accordance with
AAR Office Manual Rule 112.H are submitted using the CRB Electronic Exceptions System. When a paid
invoice exception letter is received, the billing party will respond to each exception letter with a
Response Indicator of A (Agree), P (Partially Agree) or D (Disagree). Response Indicators A and P will be
accompanied by a Detail Source EZ, indicating a CBA issued for that specific exception. Additionally, an
Amount Indicator of C (Credit) or D (Debit) will accompany a Detail Source EZ to indicate if the amount
for that exception item is a debit or credit to the receiving party.

Car Repair Billing Procedures Manual                                                                 220

When an exception letter response message contains at least 1 Detail Source EZ, a singular electronic
CBA with Detail Source CA, must be included in the response. The electronic CBA is in response to that
specific exception letter and does not preclude the billed party from submitting additional exception
letters in the future for unresolved exceptions. The electronic CBA will be included as a Format 3 Record
within the exception response message and should be the last record in the message. All electronic CBA
messages must be formatted in accordance with Y.2.4.
Y.2.2  Issuing an MID Inspection CBA
In accordance with AAR Rule 120.B, “Whenever the AAR finds that a specific AAR Interchange Rule(s) has
been violated, the Executive Director must direct the payment of refunds or adjustments of
responsibility.” When this occurs, an electronic CBA will be submitted by the responsible party using
Detail Source MI, indicating a CBA issued as a result of an MID Inspection. MID CBAs will be standalone
CBA messages and require no request or exception from the receiving party. Additionally, MID CBA
messages will not support responses to the CBA in the CRB Electronic Exception System.
Y.2.3  Issuing a Circular Letter CBA
In accordance with AAR Rule 112.D, an AAR Circular Letter may be issued that entitles an affected party
to a collection of monies. Once the affected party has satisfied their responsibility in Rule 112.D.2, an
electronic CBA will be submitted by the responsible party using Detail Source CL, indicating a CBA issued
as a result of an AAR Circular Letter claim. CL CBAs will be standalone CBA messages and will not support
responses to the CBA in the CRB Electronic Exception System.
Y.2.4  MID and Circular Letter CBA Naming Convention
Because a CBA with Detail Source CA is included in the exception letter response message, no specific
naming convention is required. However, for CBAs issued with Detail Source MI or CL, a specific file
naming convention is needed to assist in identifying when a MID or Circular Letter CBA is submitted and
display the file correctly in the dashboard. The CBA message and the .zip container will both use the
same naming convention as follows:
For MID CBAs:
SubmitterMark_ReceiverMark_MI_CBAReferenceNumber_YYMM.zip
For CL CBAs:
SubmitterMark_ReceiverMark_CL_CBAReferenceNumber_YYMM.zip
<u>Examples of correctly named CBA zip files: </u>
ABCD_WXYZ_MI_CBA123456789098_2505.zip
ABCD_WXYZ_CL_CBA987654321234_2505.zip





Car Repair Billing Procedures Manual    221

<u>Example of CBA file inside of zip file including supporting attachments:</u>
ABCD_WXYZ_MI_ CBA123456789098_2505.zip (.dat or .txt for FTP, .xlsx for CRB upload)
Attachment_1.pdf
Attachment_2.pdf
Attachment_3.pdf

Invoice Validations (EXI) – Validations between Record Format 3 (CBA) and Record Format 2
(exception letter response)
 Message Field              Error   Description
                            Code
 Billing/Issuing Party      EXI010  Check that Billing Party matches exception response
 Billed/Receiving Party     EXI011  Check that Billed Party matches exception response
 Invoice Number             EXI012  Check invoice number matches exception response
 Account Date               EXI013  Check account date matches response
 CBA Total Amount           EXI014  Check total amount is equal to the sum of EZ in the response

Appendix Z           Sample Documents
This appendix contains sample documents.
Z.1  Invoice Template
The following is a sample invoice that can be used as a template for sending monthly AAR Car Repair
invoices from the Billing Party to the Billed Party.
COMPANY LOGO
             INVOICING PARTY
 ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE FOR AAR CAR REPAIRS
<INVOICE NUMBER>
<INVOICING/BILLING PARTY>

(BILLED PARTY INITIAL)                                INVOICE NO.: <INVOICE NUMBER>
BILLED PARTY COMPANY NAME                             ACCOUNT DT: YY/MM
ADDRESS 1                                             INVOICE DT: YYYY/MM/DD
ADDRESS 2
CITY, STATE, ZIP CODE, COUNTRY

REPAIRS TO CARS IN ACCORDANCE WITH AAR RULES
                              CHARGES
 Defect Card (DC)                       $50,030.13
 Billing Repair Card (BR)     $1,209,749.15
 <Add'l CRB Categories>                             $0
 TOTAL INVOICE AMOUNT         $1,259,779.28





PLEASE REMIT TO THE FOLLOWING ADDRESS     DIRECT INQUIRIES TO:
WITH REFERENCE TO ABOVE BILL:             TITLE
INVOICING PARTY COMPANY                   INVOICING PARTY COMPANY
<IP REMIT TO ADDRESS INFO>                <IP INQUIRIES INFO>


DIRECT EXCEPTIONS TO:
TITLE
INVOICING PARTY COMPANY
<IP EXCEPTION HANDLING INFO>
